- **ralph**: Ralph is an autonomous multi-model iteration loop that applies the fresh-context pattern to idea generation. - **ralphctl-abstraction-first**: Cross-phase skill — design the shape of the change (entities, boundaries, seams) before generating code, tasks, or acceptance criteria. Failure mode is "big blob" output that obscures the core change. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-abstraction-first) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-alignment**: Cross-phase skill — establish a shared understanding of what will and will not be done before producing output. Restates the input back to the user, surfaces assumptions, names non-goals, and reaches agreement before drafting output. Use before writing criteria, tasks, or code whenever the input's scope has not yet been confirmed. For an input that is still a raw, unshaped idea needing multiple candidate directions before one is chosen, run the ralphctl-idea-refinement skill first when it is installed in this session; alignment then confirms whichever direction comes out of it. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-alignment) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-api-and-interface-design**: Interface-design discipline for any surface one piece of code exposes to another — network endpoints, library exports, module boundaries, CLI flags and exit codes, file and message formats. Contract first, one consistent error model, validation at the trust boundary, additive change over breaking change, and every observable behaviour treated as a commitment (Hyrum's law). Use when designing a new interface, changing an existing one, or planning work that crosses a module or service boundary. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-api-and-interface-design) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-cherny-workflow**: Session-discipline skill — settle the plan before touching code, work in small verified increments with a feedback loop that proves each change works, and record corrections so the same mistake is not repeated. Use when executing multi-step coding tasks where batching unverified work would compound risk; bundles plan-first sequencing and learning capture on top of the narrower per-change habits in ralphctl-iterative-review and (when installed) ralphctl-karpathy-guidelines. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-cherny-workflow) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-code-review-and-quality**: Multi-phase code-quality skill — primary frame for the evaluator role in Execute, the architecture axis in Plan, and correctness/readability in Refine. Multi-axis code review with severity vocabulary. Use when acting as the evaluator assessing a generator's output, and when reviewing any change before signalling completion. AI-written code needs MORE scrutiny, not less. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-code-review-and-quality) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-code-simplification**: Behaviour-preserving simplification of existing code — understand why the code exists before changing it, then reduce nesting, unclear names, duplication, dead code, and needless indirection one step at a time with the existing tests unchanged and green after each. Use when a task asks to refactor or clean up working code, or when code you just made work is heavier than it needs to be; for keeping a new change small from the start, see ralphctl-surgical-simplicity and ralphctl-ponytail when installed. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-code-simplification) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-codebase-design**: Shared vocabulary and tests for module design — deep modules (much behaviour behind a small interface), seams placed where behaviour actually varies, the deletion test for pass-through layers, and the interface as the test surface. Use when planning module boundaries, deciding where a seam or abstraction belongs, judging whether a layer earns its keep, or making code easier to test through its interface. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-codebase-design) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-debugging-and-error-recovery**: Systematic root-cause debugging. Use when tests fail, builds break, something that worked before broke, or behaviour does not match expectations — when you need to figure out what broke and why. Follow stop-the-line → reproduce → localize → reduce → root-cause → guard-with-regression-test → verify, not guessing; the reproduction and regression steps follow the same red-green discipline as ralphctl-test-driven-development. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-debugging-and-error-recovery) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-deprecation-and-migration**: Discipline for removing or replacing code, interfaces, stored data shapes, and dependencies safely — confirm the replacement covers every consumer, migrate consumers incrementally behind an adapter or switch, change persisted data in additive expand-then-contract phases with a tested way back, and remove the old path only once nothing uses it. Use when retiring an API, module, feature, flag, or dependency, renaming or dropping a stored field, or moving callers from one implementation to another. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-deprecation-and-migration) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-domain-driven-design**: Domain-modelling skill — align code with the business domain through ubiquitous language, bounded contexts, small aggregates, domain events, and core-domain focus. Use when naming concepts, drawing module or service boundaries, deciding entity vs value object, integrating external systems, or when the code no longer matches how the business talks about itself. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-domain-driven-design) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-frontend-ui-engineering**: UI engineering discipline for user-facing interfaces — follow the project's design system and tokens instead of a generic look, handle loading, empty, error, and permission states, meet accessibility basics (keyboard access, labels, focus, contrast, never colour alone), and keep components focused with data loading separate from presentation. Use when building or changing screens, components, layouts, or interaction states in any UI stack. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-frontend-ui-engineering) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-humanizer**: Edit prose so it reads like a specific person wrote it while keeping its meaning intact — remove staged contrasts, restating closers, run-up openers, reflexive lists of three, dash-chained clauses, inflated significance, promotional and stock model vocabulary, decorative bold and headings, and chat leftovers, while keeping every fact and the format the output contract requires. Use when writing or revising human-facing text such as a PR description, requirements prose, or a summary. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-humanizer) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-idea-refinement**: Ideation skill — refine a raw, unshaped idea into a sharp, buildable concept through divergent expansion (variation lenses like inversion, simplification, audience shift) followed by convergent stress-testing (user value, feasibility, differentiation), ending in a one-pager with explicit assumptions and a "Not Doing" list. Use when an idea is still vague and multiple directions should be widened before narrowing; once a direction is already concrete, confirming it is the shorter restate-and-agree pass in ralphctl-alignment, not a full ideation cycle. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-idea-refinement) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-iterative-review**: Cross-phase skill — treat AI output as a controlled feedback loop, not a one-shot generation. Run the cheap check after each meaningful change, then re-read the output once more before signalling completion. For the fuller session workflow — plan-first sequencing plus durable learning capture — see the opt-in ralphctl-cherny-workflow skill. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-iterative-review) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-karpathy-guidelines**: Behavioural guardrails against four empirical LLM coding failure modes — silent assumptions, over-complication, orthogonal damage to unfamiliar code, and declaring done without verification. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to surface assumptions early, keep changes proportionate to the task, and loop until explicit success criteria pass; broader than the implementation-choice ladder in ralphctl-ponytail or the scope checklist in ralphctl-surgical-simplicity (when those are installed), which it complements rather than duplicates. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-karpathy-guidelines) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-minimal-scaffolding**: Cross-phase skill — question every piece of AI-workflow scaffolding on every model upgrade; remove non-load-bearing pieces one at a time with measurement. Complexity drifts upward by default; subtraction requires discipline. Governs scaffolding built around AI usage (prompts, wrapper scripts, guardrails, validation steps), not the task's application code — for code-level minimalism defer to the ralphctl-ponytail, ralphctl-surgical-simplicity, and ralphctl-karpathy-guidelines skills when installed in this session. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-minimal-scaffolding) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-observability-and-instrumentation**: Instrument code so its production behaviour can be diagnosed from the outside — start from the questions an operator will ask, then emit structured events with stable names, a correlation id, and an entry-point field, bounded-cardinality metrics with latency percentiles, traces across async boundaries, and symptom-based alerts, never secrets or personal data. Use when adding I/O, retries, queues, background jobs, external integrations, or endpoints, or when a reported problem could not be diagnosed from existing telemetry. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-observability-and-instrumentation) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-performance-optimization**: Measure-first performance discipline — take a baseline with the project's own tooling, find the actual bottleneck, change one thing, re-measure the same way, and keep the change only when it beats run-to-run noise with the tests still green. Use when a task states a performance target, reports slowness or a regression, or touches a hot path, query, cache, or connection pool; not for speculative optimisation without evidence of a problem. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-performance-optimization) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-ponytail**: Anti-over-engineering ladder for choosing HOW to implement something — the smallest change that fully solves the task. Before writing custom code, climb the rungs in order and stop at the first that holds. Does this need to exist at all (YAGNI) → already in the codebase → standard library or platform feature → already-installed dependency → one line → minimum code that works. Use when picking an implementation approach or evaluating a new dependency; for keeping the footprint of an already-chosen approach small, defer to the ralphctl-surgical-simplicity skill when installed. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-ponytail) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-security-and-hardening**: Security posture for code that crosses a trust boundary — threat-model first, then validate untrusted input, keep secrets out of code and logs, check authorization, guard destructive operations on derived paths, review new dependencies, minimise personal data, and treat model output as untrusted. Use when writing or reviewing code that handles external input, authentication, sensitive data, file-system targets, third-party services, dependencies, or LLM output; it goes deeper than the security axis of ralphctl-code-review-and-quality (when installed), which it complements. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-security-and-hardening) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-source-driven-development**: Ground framework- and library-specific decisions in the version the project actually uses — read the resolved version from the manifest and lockfile, check local truth first (installed package sources, type declarations, bundled docs, existing usage) and then official documentation for that version, follow the documented pattern, and record the source or flag the decision as unverified. Use when writing or planning code against a framework, library, SDK, or platform API whose correct usage depends on the version. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-source-driven-development) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-spec-driven-development**: Requirements discipline for sharpening the ticket or idea a requirements session already produces into a real spec — a clear objective, explicit boundaries, testable success criteria, surfaced assumptions, and named open questions, kept in sync as decisions change. Use when a ticket or idea is vague, bundles several capabilities, or states goals that cannot yet be checked. Adds no files and no approval steps of its own; the session's requirements body is the spec and its approval gate is the review. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-spec-driven-development) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-surgical-simplicity**: Execute-phase scope-containment checklist — write the minimum code the task needs and touch only the files and lines the task requires; surface out-of-scope findings as notes rather than fixing them inline. Governs the footprint of a change whose approach is already decided; for choosing that implementation approach itself, defer to the ralphctl-ponytail skill when installed. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-surgical-simplicity) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-test-driven-development**: Execute-phase skill — drive changes with the red-green-refactor loop; write the failing test before the code that makes it pass; for bug fixes, this is the reproduction test itself. Use for any logic change, bug fix, or behavioural modification; for the full root-cause triage pipeline around an unexpected failure, see ralphctl-debugging-and-error-recovery. ([lukas-grigis/ralphctl](https://github.com/lukas-grigis/ralphctl/tree/HEAD/src/integration/ai/skills/bundled/ralphctl-test-driven-development) · ★24)
- **ralphctl-writing-for-agents**: How to write documents an AI agent consumes — project context files (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, and similar), skills, task specs, and requirements.
